- `packages/ui/src/ticket/commit-files.ts`: the Commit and push file list: the uncommitted tree as entries, the Changed and New groups, the ticked paths (both paths of a rename), and a long list folded by folder. Key symbols: `entriesOf`, `commitGroups`, `tickedPaths`, `commitRows`, `COMMIT_FOLD_OVER`.
- `packages/ui/src/ticket/CommitComposer.svelte`: Commit, one of the three actions of the Commit and push panel: the message box, the two message buttons with the model and cost the pass reported (`written`), every file not committed with a tick in a Changed and a New group, all ticked at first, a long list folded by folder with a tick per folder (`commitRows` in `commit-files.ts`), and Commit N files, off at none, a direct git action with its own pending state that sends exactly the ticked paths (`tickedPaths`). `HooksSwitch` above Commit says the git hooks are skipped and offers Run hooks for one commit. Key symbols: `files`, `oncommit`, `onwrite`, `written`, `hooks`, `count`.
- `packages/ui/src/ticket/HooksSwitch.svelte`: the line "Git hooks are skipped, because an agent wrote these changes." and the Run hooks tick, off by default, used by `CommitComposer` and the push dialog of `PublishPanel`. Key symbols: `checked`.
- `packages/ui/src/ticket/PublishPanel.svelte`: Push and Create pull request, two of the three independent actions of the Commit and push panel, with no step numbers: push ("Push 2 commits to origin/<branch>", on by the commits the remote has not got, `ahead`, else off with its reason, `idle`) with a `Help` tip on what it does, pull, and "Create pull request", a link once the branch is pushed, with "Copy link" beside it (`copyLink`, through the page's `oncopy`) in place of the printed compare URL and, before the push, a tip on why it waits. Push opens the push dialog on every push: branch, remote and change chips, a line that files not committed stay in the folder, the `HooksSwitch` tick (reset on every open), Push N commits, Not yet. A push commits nothing. Key symbols: `onpush`, `onpull`, `uncommitted`, `ahead`, `idle`, `copyLink`.
- `packages/ui/src/ticket/patch.ts`: a patch as lines, and as lines with both numbers read off the hunk headers (`numberedLines`). Key symbols: `patchLines`, `numberedLines`, `NumberedLine`.
- `packages/ui/src/ticket/repo-id.ts`: safe id suffix of a group. Key symbols: `repoSuffix`.
- `packages/ui/src/ticket/DiscussionTab.svelte`: the Discussion tab, with no frame, only the divider beside the threads: the thread list in the board's phase order (`order`, the greyed phases in place), the open one a soft filled row, the ask discussions that hold something, then the drawn Ask row that opens a new one (`fresh`, `askHref`), each with its count, session and when its run started in the server's words (`said`); the open thread with the person's and the agent's messages in order, a run line above the first message of each run (`dividerLine`), each agent message with its run's tool calls as folded rows that open to their input and result (folded under one line past `CALLS_SHOWN`), a skeleton line while they stream (`toolsWaiting`), a line when the run's log is gone, and what the run spent. The composer names the engine and effort as plain text and offers only the model as a chip that opens the models of the run's engine (`picked`, `choose`), because a resume stays on its engine. The box has no resize grip, and the resume command is a copy button, not printed. Send is the amber go button with the estimate only where runs measured one, and an empty message is refused with a sentence. An ask thread is a read-only session: its first message starts it (amber Send, the Ask model, what a new Ask session takes). Send holds while `asking`, in a phase thread too, and is `busy` from the press until the run ends, with pulsing `dots` in it and an answering row closing the conversation. The tab opens on the newest message a frame after it draws (the router scrolls to the top first) and follows what grows only while the page is at its end (`section`, `nearBottom`). The thread list is sticky, capped to the window with its own scroll, and static above the conversation on a narrow window. The person's avatar is a head and shoulders. An ask discussion is listed and headed by its subject, else its opening, else its label (`named`), and has a three dot menu button (`openMenu`, `closeMenu`, the menu button pattern) with Rename, which edits the name in place and saves through `onrename` (`startRename`, `saveName`). A PR comment thread is the same, with its own line that its answers never change the row. A message with `forNext` shows a For <column> tag, and the phase thread's head then says such an answer goes to no agent there. Key symbols: `onsend`, `oncopy`, `estimates`, `CALLS_SHOWN`, `asking`, `busy`, `dots`, `nearBottom`, `order`, `dividerLine`, `choose`.
- `packages/ui/src/ticket/follow.ts`: whether the page is at or near its end (`nearBottom`, within `FOLLOW_SLACK`), so the Discussion tab follows a new message only then. Key symbols: `nearBottom`, `FOLLOW_SLACK`.
- `packages/ui/src/ticket/LogTab.svelte`: the Log tab: the runs in a list down the left of the log, newest on top, runs of one phase in a row under one heading (`runGroups`), each with its attempt number where the phase ran more than once, its state with `runTag` and its start as a time ago (`Ago`), the shown row scrolled into sight inside the list, the list no taller than the window below it, a green check on a done run and an amber dot beside the live one's link (never on it, `amber.test.ts`), and below 640px of tab width (a container query, as the app rail takes part of the window) the same runs in one select whose GET form goes to `?run=`, what the shown run ran on, the wall that held it (`wallLabel`) and when it started, "Hide tool calls" (`withoutTools`), the rows drawn with `LogLine` and timed from the run's start, and the sticky footer: the state pill, `runTotals`, Stop read off the snapshot card (`runView`, only while the card's own run is the one shown, held until the card moves) and Download raw. Key symbols: `logRows`, `LogLine`, `runGroups`, `runTotals`, `runTag`, `runView`, `stopRun`.
- `packages/ui/src/ticket/LogLine.svelte`: one log row, the Log tab's and the peek sheet's (`compact`: the time of day only where the log noted one, the short kind words, one line): the time since the start, the kind word (`LOG_WORDS` in the full log), the text, a tool's subject beside its name (a "says" row rendered with `Markdown`), a tool's input folded, a figure at the edge (a result's size), the retry wait in a neutral box, and the edge of a retry, a limit, a refusal, a question or a failure. Key symbols: `KIND_WORDS`, `LOG_WORDS`, `compact`, `Markdown`.
- `packages/ui/src/ticket/log.ts`: log rows from engine neutral events, each timed from the run's start where the route sent `at` (`TimedEvent`, `sinceStart`), a "says" row without the closing marker or a question line, a result's lines and its size in words (`byteSize`), which the Discussion tab's tool rows use too, the kind column's words (`KIND_WORDS` short for the peek sheet, `LOG_WORDS` for the full log), a tool's subject the route read (`subject`), the time of day for the peek sheet (`clockTime`, through `logRows`' clock), and a run as the Log tab lists it (`LogRun`) with its footer figures (`runTotals`: elapsed, and tokens and cost only where reported, with `unpriced` on an ended run with no cost) its tag in the run list (`runTag`) and the list's phase groups and attempt numbers (`runGroups`), a triage's counted as runs and not attempts (`runCount`). A refused plain Bash command with a variable in front names the allowed command that would take it (`variableHint`). Key symbols: `logRows`, `retryLine`, `byteSize`, `KIND_WORDS`, `LOG_WORDS`, `clockTime`, `LogRun`, `ENDED_RUN`, `runTotals`, `runTag`, `runGroups`, `runCount`, `withoutTools`, `TimedEvent`, `sinceStart`.
- `packages/ui/src/ticket/PrsTab.svelte`: the PRs tab: `PrLinks` on top, then Comments with when they were read and the counts, Read comments with its own pending line and the gh sentence in red, one row per discussion (unread dot with the word for a screen reader, ref, first line, reviewer, message count, verdict chip once triage gives one, status, path:line for a thread, GitHub link) that opens to every message drawn by `Markdown` with Mark done, the folded "Nothing to action" footer with one way back per row (Reopen on a done row, Needs action on one triage said none to), focus moved to the row where it lands after a mark (or the folded footer's summary), and the empty states. Run triage (Triage again once a verdict is in) with what it runs on and costs and why it is held, the refused triage file in red, and on each row the summary and the drafted reply with Copy, the reason once the row opens. `onopen` marks the messages seen as it mounts. With `onfix` each row gets Fix (Fix again when fixed, none when done) and Discuss, the Fix held with its reason (`fixView`) and off the snapshot after a press (`fixPressed`), and a line for fixing, "Fix asks a question" with the question (`fixAsks`, answered on the Overview), fixed ("Fixed, commit and push from Changes", `changesHref`, with the note) or "Fix failed" with the reason. Discuss holds while its write goes (`discussing`), and the pressed Fix is busy until the board answers (`fixRef`). Key symbols: `onread`, `onmark`, `onopen`, `ontriage`, `oncopy`, `onfix`, `ondiscuss`, `fixPressed`, `runTriage`, `toggle`.
- `packages/ui/src/ticket/prs.ts`: the PRs tab's rules: a message's first meaningful line (`firstLine`, past quotes, hidden HTML comments, fences and lone tags), the reviewer, unread, the footer split (`splitRows`: done or verdict none, unless the person pulled it back), the GitHub link of a row (`discussionLink`) "Read just now" (`readWords`), the Run triage button's label and held reason (`triageView`), and a row's Fix button (`fixView`: Fix again on a fixed row, held while any row is fixing (waiting for the answer when that fix asks), a run or a question holds the card, after a failure and after a press), and the board card's review comments line (`feedbackLine`). Key symbols: `feedbackLine`, `triageView`, `fixView`, `firstLine`, `reviewerOf`, `unread`, `splitRows`, `discussionLink`, `readWords`, `PrDiscussion`.
- `packages/ui/src/ticket/merge.ts`: the words of why a pull request cannot merge: the chip label per reason (`BLOCKER_LABEL`), the card page line with its links (`blockerLine`, `blockerText`), the board chip (`blockerChip`) and the pull requests the card page names (`mergeShown`). `fixCiOf` is Fix CI by the server's rule: the Implement stage with the card's models, or null, and the refusal for a busy card, no readable log or an engine that cannot start. Key symbols: `BLOCKER_LABEL`, `blockerLine`, `blockerText`, `blockerChip`, `mergeShown`, `MergePull`, `RUNNING_WORDS`, `fixCiOf`, `FixCi`, `FIX_CI_LABEL`, `FIX_CI_NOTE`.
- `packages/ui/src/ticket/PrLinks.svelte`: the top of the PRs tab: the card's pull requests, each linked with its title and its state as the last read saw it, Detach on an attached one, which asks first in a `Dialog` naming the pull request and how many discussions on the card go with it (`losesLine`; `PrsTab` counts them per pull request), and gives focus to the link field once the row is gone, and "Found by the branch, so it stays" on the others, and the attach field with its own pending line, the name as the list shows it once attached and the refusal sentence in red. Key symbols: `attach`, `detach`, `losesLine`, `closed`.
- `packages/ui/src/ticket/ReviewTab.svelte`: the left column of a pull request card's Review tab: the round selector with each round's count, the decision, the author and the head the round read, then one row per finding with its label, its place, its decoration and the round that raised it (`ageWord`: "new in round 2", "open since round 1"), Copy per block, the fixed ones folded into one native disclosure line (`fixedLine`), and the round file with a link to the Files tab. It moves nothing: the next round is the decision panel's amber button beside it, Agent pre-review on a card back on Incoming. Each row shows the place (`blockPlace`) beside the comment, the subject over the discussion, and Copy hands over the comment alone. - `packages/protocol/src/review-round.ts`: the round file format, read once for the Review tab, Copy all findings and the output check. A block is its place on a line of its own (`path:line` or `path:start-end`), the conventional comment's label line (nine labels, decorations in brackets, a subject), a blank line and the discussion; a round from before the place line still reads, one block per paragraph. `isBlocking` is an issue or a todo not marked non-blocking, or any comment marked blocking; the summary's verdict follows it. Key symbols: `parseRound`, `normaliseRound`, `ROUND_LABELS`, `ROUND_DECORATIONS`, `ReviewBlock`, `RoundRead`, `blockPlace`, `isBlocking`.
- `packages/ui/src/ticket/review.ts`: the rounds compared, over the protocol's round reader, which it exports again. `diffRounds` walks every round oldest first and matches a finding by its label and file, the nearest line winning, never by its place in the list: the newest round's findings with the round that raised each, and the fixed ones with the round they went in (praise is never fixed). `conventionalComments` is Copy all findings' text, each comment under its place, and `rereviewWhy` the decision panel's line on why another round makes sense. Key symbols: `parseRound`, `diffRounds`, `ageWord`, `fixedLine`, `conventionalComments`, `rereviewWhy`, `ROUND_LABELS`, `DECISION_WORD`.
- `packages/ui/src/ticket/SettingsTab.svelte`: the card's Settings tab: "This card follows Heavy on Cursor" (`title`, through `presetTitle`, or a saved preset's own name) with how many values it set itself ("With 2 values of its own", `ownValues`) and the preset switch, Light, Balanced and Heavy then the project's saved presets (`choices`), each named with its engine for screen readers and what a card costs on it where this project's runs measured one (`presets`), which clears the card's own values and names each step that lost one; then one row per step of the card's board, and under "Other runs on this card" Ask, the plain summary and any project action the card set (`CARD_RUNS`): one `ModelPicker` in `columns` under the heads Step, Engine, Model and Effort (the `thead` row, which also heads Source and "Per card"), "From the preset", or a neutral tinted changed row with Reset and what the preset would use, or a red row whose step will not start in the board's own words (`goneProblem` for a gone model) and no button, since a pick in its red picker is the fix; a switch or a Reset redraws the pickers (`drawn`) so none keeps a stale saved note, and under the head "Per card" the step's figure over this project's runs only where measured (`stepEstimate`), empty on a row that will not start. Under 760px the heads go and a row stacks: the name, the three selects, the note, then the estimate beside the button. Nothing amber. Then the tick "Go on to Implement after Plan" where the page hands one over (`chain`, `onchain`), then the repositories. Key symbols: `onpick`, `onreset`, `onpreset`, `onrepos`, `onchain`, `ModelPicker`, `choices`, `title`, `CARD_RUNS`, `goneProblem`, `switchSaid`, `drawn`.
- `packages/ui/src/ticket/ModelPicker.svelte`: the one combined picker of a step, the decision panel's, the Settings tab's and Settings, Models' (exported from the barrel, with `presetLabel`): three native selects drawn in place, engine, model and effort, compact like the card page's chips but in the text font, so nothing opens first and a narrow row wraps them; with `columns` the three take three columns of the row's grid (a subgrid), so a table lines them up under its heads, and under 760px they stack full width; each select at the width of its words, never squeezed (an engine or model pick writes both: a new engine takes the row's `presetPicks` model and effort when given, else keeps the model it offers, or takes its first; an engine marked `effortInName` shows "in model name" on one line in place of the effort select; `describedby` ties the row's sentence, such as why its model is gone, to the model select), with its own pending line, whose words on a save are `saved` ("Saved for this card." unless the screen says otherwise). A refused pick puts the select back to what is stored. A gone model and a login this machine lost stay on it, marked. A profile with no models is a closed engine option, and a line links to Settings, Engines (`enginesHref`), unless `emptyHelp` is off. The selects carry `data-pick` and an accessible name such as "Plan model". Key symbols: `onpick`, `tone`, `pickEngine`, `saved`, `enginesHref`, `emptyHelp`, `presetPicks`, `columns`.
- `packages/ui/src/ticket/card-settings.ts`: the Settings tab's words: Balanced for the stored `default`, a changed row, what the preset would use ("Heavy uses opus · high", or "The project uses" for a step set in Settings, Models), what Reset did, how many values the card set (`ownValues`, an engine with its model counts once), and the one line a preset switch says. Key symbols: `presetLabel`, `isChanged`, `ownValues`, `fromProject`, `presetUses`, `resetSaid`, `followsSaid`, `switchSaid`.
- `packages/ui/src/ticket/file-rows.ts`: the Files tab's rows of one group: the group's own files listed (folded into one row past `FOLD_OVER`), and every folder right under the group folded into one row with its files and size. `foldOf` names the folded row that holds a file. Key symbols: `fileRows`, `foldOf`, `FOLD_OVER`, `FileRow`.
- `packages/ui/src/ticket/json-tree.ts`: the Files viewer's JSON tree, pure: `isJsonFile`, `readJson` (null for text that is not JSON), the kind of each value for its color, the children by name or place, the count a folded node shows, how many levels start open (`JSON_OPEN_LEVELS`) and how many children draw before Show more (`JSON_PAGE`). Each number keeps the text the file holds (`JsonNumber`, from the reviver's source), so a 64-bit id keeps its digits; where the browser hands no source, `lossy` opens the file on Raw. Key symbols: `isJsonFile`, `readJson`, `JsonNumber`, `JSON_PAGE`, `jsonKind`, `jsonEntries`, `jsonCount`, `jsonLeaf`, `JSON_OPEN_LEVELS`.
- `packages/ui/src/ticket/JsonView.svelte`: a JSON file in the viewer: the tree with a Tree and Raw switch (`raw`, Raw first when `lossy`), or, for text that does not parse, the plain text under a line that says so. Key symbols: `parsed`, `raw`.
- `packages/ui/src/ticket/JsonNode.svelte`: one node of the JSON tree, itself again for each child: a native `<details>` for an object or an array, open on the root only, its children drawn only while open and `JSON_PAGE` at a time with a Show more press, and a leaf colored by its kind (green, blue, violet, muted, never amber). Key symbols: `JsonNode`, `open`.
- `packages/ui/src/ticket/FilesTab.svelte`: the Files tab: the files grouped by phase folder, each group badged share or internal off `CardFileGroup.share`, each row with its size (`byteSize`) and time. Every folder right under a group, and a group's own files past 50, is one row with its count and size (`fileRows` in `file-rows.ts`), folded at first and open on a press, `aria-expanded` on it; a file opened from a link opens its folder (`unfolded`, `foldOf`), and a press folds it again. A group's cache folders are lines with their count that open nothing. No outer border, only the divider. Every row opens the viewer: a file it cannot show says "No preview for this kind of file.", or that it is too big (`CardFile.tooBig`), beside Download in the head. A `.json` file draws in `JsonView`. The preview header names the file, its size and the phase that wrote it, with Copy on a text file (`oncopy`, the page touches the clipboard) and a plain Download. `start` opens one file first, from the `file` query the Overview's links and the QA thumbnails set, and only a file the listing holds, so a path out of the card folder opens nothing; without it the tab opens `plan/plan.md`, else the first file the viewer can show. A file fills the viewer up to 120ch, the shared Markdown cap. An `html` file opens drawn in an `<iframe sandbox="">` on the file route, on a light background, with a Preview and Source switch (`source`, Preview first, Source is the text) and Open in a new tab. Key symbols: `fileUrl`, `byteSize`, `writer`, `start`, `oncopy`, `source`.

### Other components and rules

- `packages/ui/src/Keyboard.svelte`: the one key handler. Key symbols: `matchKey`.
- `packages/ui/src/keymap.ts`: every key of the client in one list, in four scopes (`global`, `board`, `card`, `knowledge`), the matcher, the help sections. A board's help adds the Delivery card's tabs (`CARD_TABS`). The G chords that open something over the page rather than go to a place are `go:project` (the selector) and `go:notifications` (the bell's panel). Key symbols: `KEYS`, `CARD_TABS`, `scoped`, `tabKeys`, `liveKeys`, `matchKey`, `readPress`, `helpSections`, `hintsOf`, `capsOf`, `Binding`.
- `packages/ui/src/KeyCaps.svelte`: key caps. `bare` drops the "or" between ways, as the peek row draws J K. Key symbols: `combos`, `bare`.
- `packages/ui/src/Kbd.svelte`: one cap. Key symbols: `key`.
- `packages/ui/src/ToastHost.svelte`: draws the toast stack, keyed by each toast, and closes each one by itself: its bar's CSS animation (`.life`, `--life` from `toastLife`) is the countdown, paused by hover and focus inside, kept with reduced motion, and its end closes the toast (`expire`). Key symbols: `toasts`, `ondismiss`, `expire`, `toastLife`.
- `packages/ui/src/Toast.svelte`: one toast. Key symbols: `message`, `fix`.
- `packages/ui/src/toasts.ts`: the toast stack rules, and the run line under a toast with the engine first (`runLine`). `toastLife` is how long a toast stays: `TOAST_MS`, or `TOAST_LONG_MS` for an error or a toast with a link. Key symbols: `pushToast`, `dropToast`, `TOAST_LIMIT`, `ToastItem`, `runLine`, `toastLife`, `TOAST_MS`, `TOAST_LONG_MS`.
- `packages/ui/src/StatePill.svelte`: the state word and icon, never color alone, a check for a closed card (`check`). Key symbols: `state`, `check`.
- `packages/ui/src/Button.svelte`: buttons; `go` is the one amber variant, only where a run starts. Key symbols: `variant`.
- `packages/ui/src/Banner.svelte`: a banner with an icon per kind. Key symbols: `kind`.
- `packages/ui/src/InfoBox.svelte`: the explanation of a page under its title, a full-width note in the blue information tint with the info icon and a few short lines (`role="note"`, not a status). Tested in `packages/ui/src/InfoBox.mount.test.ts`. Key symbols: `children`.
- `packages/ui/src/Skeleton.svelte`: grey placeholder shapes for a part on its way: `line` (`width`), `block` (`height`), `rows` (`count` rows of dot, name and meta in the Tools row grid), `list` (a name over a meta line, for a side column), `text` (the lines of a paragraph, the last one short), `card` (a title line and two short lines) and `pill` (an inline count). Seen only after 200ms, so a fast answer does not flash grey. A soft pulse, none under reduced motion. The shapes are `aria-hidden` and the caller's `label` is read instead. Tested in `packages/ui/src/Skeleton.mount.test.ts`. Key symbols: `shape`, `label`, `count`.
- `packages/ui/src/HelpTip.svelte`: a "?" beside a label that opens a native `popover="auto"` box (in the top layer, so no table or panel clips it; the browser shuts it on Esc or a click outside) with a few sentences, an optional `example` and docs link (`href`, `linkText`). `aria-expanded`, `aria-controls` and the text as `aria-describedby` on the button, any Esc kept from the page's Back while open, and placed at the "?" by `placePopover`, upward when there is no room below. Tested in `packages/ui/src/HelpTip.mount.test.ts`. Key symbols: `label`, `text`, `example`, `href`, `placePopover`.
- `packages/ui/src/popover.ts`: where a popover goes beside its button: below, or above when the room below is too short and above fits, kept inside the screen sideways. Tested in `packages/ui/src/popover.test.ts`. Key symbols: `placePopover`.
- `packages/ui/src/ErrorBox.svelte`: a problem, its output and the fix. Key symbols: `fix`.
- `packages/ui/src/Help.svelte`: a round "?" that holds an explanation, open on hover, focus or a tap, shut by Esc (which it keeps from the page's Back) or by moving away. The text is the button's `aria-describedby`, so it is read while shut. The tip opens leftward under the "?", or rightward with `align="start"` for a "?" near the left edge. Tested in `packages/ui/src/Help.mount.test.ts`. Key symbols: `text`, `label`, `align`.
- `packages/ui/src/Chip.svelte`: a chip; `mono` for evidence. Key symbols: `mono`.
- `packages/ui/src/Switch.svelte`: a switch. Key symbols: `checked`.
- `packages/ui/src/CoachNote.svelte`: a note shown once, hidden while any dialog is open. Key symbols: `seen`.
- `packages/ui/src/OptionButtons.svelte`: the answers a question offers. Key symbols: `onpick`.
- `packages/ui/src/Markdown.svelte`: markdown, sanitized. Key symbols: `sanitize`.
- `packages/ui/src/markdown.ts`: the allow list. Images stay only from GitHub's upload and repository file hosts (`IMAGE_HOSTS`, `github.com/user-attachments/`) and `data:`, never camo; any other becomes its text, a link when it has an address and sits in no link (`imageAllowed`). On github.com `GITHUB_IMAGE` also takes the older upload form `<owner>/<repo>/assets/<digits>/<uuid>` and `<owner>/<repo>/raw/`, never a release file. Key symbols: `sanitize`, `imageAllowed`.
- `packages/ui/src/pips.ts`: how the card page trail draws and names each phase's run. Key symbols: `pipOf`, `runSaid`.
- `packages/ui/src/SlotsMeter.svelte`: busy slots from the snapshot. Key symbols: `slots`.
- `packages/ui/src/LaneChips.svelte`: one chip per lane. A holder of another project reads "in <name>" off `projects`. Key symbols: `lanes`, `projects`.
- `packages/ui/src/RoleGlyph.svelte`: the column role icon. Key symbols: `role`.
- `packages/ui/src/EnginesList.svelte`: the old engine check list. No screen draws it since Engines took `EngineTools`; kept as an export. Key symbols: `rows`.
- `packages/ui/src/engines.ts`: its rules, and onboarding's rule on the server's answer of which engines run every phase here (`EveryPhaseHere`): `canContinue` and `missingEngine`, which names a found engine that is not enough, what to install and the wall check that failed. Key symbols: `engineRows`, `canContinue`, `missingEngine`, `EveryPhaseHere`.
- `packages/ui/src/KnowledgeBrowser.svelte`: the Knowledge screen: the top bar with Search knowledge (`query`, filtering by name and text through `fileMatches`, focused by the route's "/" through `focusSearch`) and Optimize (the one go button, greyed from the snapshot), the files as a tree with one Tab stop (`knowledgeTree`, `role="tree"`: each folder folded with its count, grouped by repository, then the top files, open when it holds the open file, while a search runs or once the person opened it, held in `unfolded`; arrows, Home, End, Enter and Space through `treeKeys`, the Tab stop in `stop`), each with its day, "waiting for review" and a line for the paths not on the base branch, the foot with "Last Optimize" (`dayMonth`) and the short folder, the open file drawn plain with one line above it for its newest applied change (its card, its day, Revert, and a "Show the change" toggle that draws the lines inline, held per page view in `shownChange`; no toggle when the change wrote the whole file or its lines cannot be placed), and the side pane with Taught by, Depends on (each path in `paths:` with its state in words), History newest first, Learn this project while no file is open (the go button, or the question it waits on with `OptionButtons`, a reply box and Send and resume, through `learn` and `draft`), Learn your repositories below it (`repos`: a box per repository with "not learned yet", "learned", or "learning now" and "waiting to learn" (off) while a Learn run is on it, the ones not learned yet ticked at first through `picked` and `toLearn`, the amber Starts line through `startsCommand` and the estimate per repository (`learnEstimateText`), or the row's refusal, and the go button counting the ticked ones, replaced by a status line while Learn runs go (`learning`) and by "Every repository is learned" when nothing is left), Pending review with the run named (`by`) and the kept lines, Apply and Return, and the review switch. Apply all in the Pending review head while more than one run waits, posting every run on screen. Read in full opens a run in a wide `Dialog` with its lines uncapped and Apply in the footer (`opened`, `openRun`), closing once the run leaves the list. A run answered ok leaves the list at once (`answered`). With `onpost` Apply, Apply all and Return post in place (`sendHere`), and their answer prints under Pending review (`answeredHere`) and scrolls into view; without it they are plain posts and the answer prints at the top of the side pane. The change summaries in History and Pending review are Markdown, their headings from h3. A file link keeps the focus when it opens (`data-sveltekit-keepfocus`). A null `view` is the wait: the header and Search, and `Skeleton` shapes in the three panes. `running` (off the Now stream) names the knowledge run on the lock line with its time and last action, and turns the Learn this project or Optimize button into its running form with a turning mark. Key symbols: `view`, `given`, `review`, `learn`, `draft`, `repos`, `running`, `took`, `picked`, `tree`, `unfolded`, `treeKeys`, `stop`, `placed`, `shownChange`, `showing`, `query`, `fileMatches`, `focusSearch`, `dayMonth`.
- `packages/ui/src/knowledge.ts`: its rules. `knowledgeView` cuts the open file around the lines of its newest applied change (`FilePart`, `KnowledgeInline`), never inside the frontmatter or a code block and only where each line, and each line git printed around it, is still in the file. `lineGroups` sets a kept copy against the file for a change that waits. The folder is named by the short `shown` the page gives, never the long path. `pendingRuns` names the run that wrote a waiting change by its phase (`by`): a wrap-up, Optimize, Learn this project or Learn your repositories, and Last Optimize leaves Learn this project out. Each file's `paths:` read against `pathsOnBase` gives the row its gone paths and the open file its `KnowledgePath` list, worded by `PATH_WORDS`. Each row says whether a change to it waits (`pending`). `knowledgeTree` cuts the rows into the top files and one `KnowledgeFolder` per first path part, grouped by the project's repositories (`repos` on the input and the view): their folders first in the project's order, each with its `repository.md` first, then Shared (`FOLDER_LABELS`), then any other folder, with its waiting count; the top files come last, and a project of one repository gets its folder's files at the top with no folder row. Key symbols: `knowledgeView`, `knowledgeTree`, `FOLDER_LABELS`, `KnowledgeFolder`, `PATH_WORDS`, `KnowledgePath`, `pendingRuns`, `changeSource`, `lineGroups`, `codeSpans`, `fileMatches`, `dayMonth`, `FilePart`, `KnowledgeInline`.
- `packages/ui/src/project.ts`: the old wizard's project step, which no screen draws now. Key symbols: `projectView`.
- `packages/ui/src/flow.ts`: the old wizard's flow step, which no screen draws now. Key symbols: `flowView`.

## `packages/skills`

- `packages/skills/skills`: one folder per phase skill, named `nolzu-<phase>`: `nolzu-refine`, `nolzu-plan`, `nolzu-implement`, `nolzu-qa`, `nolzu-wrapup`, `nolzu-architecture`, `nolzu-design`, `nolzu-investigate`, `nolzu-research`, `nolzu-pr-review`, `nolzu-feedback`, `nolzu-optimize`, `nolzu-orient`, `nolzu-learn-repo`, `nolzu-assess`. Each has a `SKILL.md` and a `finish.md`. `nolzu-learn-repo` is Learn your repositories, one repository per run: it reads the README, the CI files and the stack files and not the code, fills that repository's file (what it is for, how to install, run, test, lint and build it) and its run recipe section, every line with where it was read, and never asks, because no screen would show the question. Each `SKILL.md` says near its top to read the project's `.nolzu/phases/<phase>.md` too when it is there, for an engine run without Nolzu, and `skills.test.ts` lets that one project path through its folder checks. `nolzu-orient` is Learn this project: it reads what people wrote (README, contributing guide, agent files, decision records, CI, manifests) and not the code, asks at most four questions one at a time with answers in `orient/question.md`, and writes at most three notes of about 60 lines, the rules as prohibitions with their reason and every line ending with `(from <file>)` or `(told by the person)`, with `orient/changes.md` for the reviewer. Refine starts `refine/refined.md` with `# <KEY>: <title>` (at most 80 characters), then `Kind:` (feature, fix or chore) and `Branch:` (two to four hyphenated words), then the review block, and gives the skeleton of the brief in its skill. Plan writes `plan/plan.md` with the review block, Approach with `Read at:`, Decisions (`D1`), the numbered steps with their fields indented under each (Files, Uses, Copy, Tests, Check, Undo, Covers) in the form of the skill's own example, which `parsePlan` reads, Risks, Order, Settled questions, Rules that apply, Not doing and Read first. On an epic, and only there, Plan also writes `share/PLAN.md` for colleagues: the plan in plain words, then `## Tasks` with one `### <n>. <title>` per step, with no internal path or id; the prompt says the card is an epic (`epicCard`, `SHARE_PLAN_FILE` in `packages/runner/src/prompt.ts`). Plan writes `qa/checks.md`, the list of what QA will check, each brief check as `AC1 (must):` with an optional `How:` line, and QA reports one row per step of it in section 2 of `qa/report.md`. The wrap-up writes its plain summary about the whole card, which a closed card shows as its one summary. It reads the card's history in the prompt to find what cost time, keeps a learning only past the bar (costly to find again, rework if lost, true in three months, checked against the code), corrects before it adds and changes one entry at a time; `shared/knowledge.md` names what earns a file (a silent trap, a contract between files, a precedent, a dead end, which file holds the truth) and what does not (a restatement of one file, a stack list, a folder tree), and gives every note frontmatter with a `description` that says when to read it, `source` (card and commit) and `paths` (tracked on the base branch, never `.tasks/`), and Optimize keeps that shape. On a Research card it reads `research/answer.md` or `investigate/report.md`, because a question or an investigation has no plan or diff. Plan is read only, so the plan prompt's write line in `packages/runner/src/prompt.ts` names that file too. The formats point at each other: refine numbers its Assumptions (`A1`), its Acceptance checks with a level and a `Check:` line (`AC1 (must):`) and at most three open questions (`Q1`) with a default and `blocks: yes|no`, `yes` when the answer changes what a person using the product sees or gets, and writes What the code does today with `Read at:`, Rules that apply and Read first; architecture writes the review block, The shape with `Read at:`, Interfaces as signatures with their error answers, The decisions as `D1:` lines with `Not:` and `Keep:`, What is still open as `Q` lines going on from the brief's, Touch points, Test surfaces, Not doing and Read first; design.md writes the review block and `## Screens` with `Read at:`, `Tokens:` and one `### <screen>.html` per screen with `Address:`, `States:` (named, with `AC` ids and exact words), `Data:`, `Reuse:` and a `Question:` in the brief's question shape; on an epic the plan folds a helper step into the step that uses it and names the `D` ids a step builds on on its Uses line; a task's Refine numbers its own checks, names the epic check each serves, and checks each carried check against the code, writing the one the code supports under My calls; each plan step carries `Covers:`; checks and report rows start with the `AC` id and level, every pass or fail row has `How:` and `Evidence:`, a failed row has `Reason:`, `Expected:` and `Actual:`, section 1 of `qa/report.md` is QA's review block, and screenshots go to `qa/evidence/` under lower-case names; `shared/verdict.md` passes a card only when every `must` criterion passed with evidence; implement keeps `implement/progress.md` as its record (`Base:`, the review block at run end, `## Steps done` with `Red:`, `Green:` or `Check:` proof under each step and `- Fix:` items, added to and never rewritten), writes `implement/deviations.md` as rulings with `Why:` and `If wrong:`, and `implement/pr.md` with `## What changed` and `## How to test`, which becomes the pull request body. Design writes one `design/<screen>.html` per screen with every state, `design/tokens.css` that each links, and `design/design.md`; plan, implement and QA read `design/` and fall back to an old epic's `mockups/`. `shared/finish.md` holds the rules for every phase: the contract (the reader rule, the `## Before you approve` review block, evidence and honesty, ownership, size, ask or decide, and one plain shell command per call with files made and changed by the file tools, never a redirect or heredoc, and scratch files in the card folder), one paragraph or list item per line in the card and knowledge folders, and one list item per surprise added to `<card folder>/discoveries.md` (a fact in at most about four sentences on one line, never a gap), which the wrap-up reads first. Research and Investigate open with the answer in one sentence and a `Confidence:` line, then (Research) Why, What this does not answer, Searched and not found, or (Investigate) What was reported, What was found, Hypotheses tried with `Result:`, The fix or next step, Searched and not found, both with an optional `## If this becomes a card` in the brief's lines (which Refine starts from) and `## Sources` last; assess adds `Confidence:` and `What would change it:` under the likely cause; pr-review reads the earlier rounds, writes a still-open issue again on its file and leaves a fixed one out, keeps to correctness and the stated intent, and writes `summary.md` as the round's review block (`Verdict:`, `Findings:`, `Earlier issues:` on a re-review, `Not reviewed:`); feedback reads `feedback/discussions.md`, keeps earlier verdicts unless a newer message changes them, and writes `feedback/triage.md` as four line blocks with one of five verdicts and a drafted reply, posting nothing. `skills.test.ts` holds the section orders and these links.
- `packages/skills/shared`: the rule text several skills share: `finish.md`, `knowledge.md`, `verdict.md`.
- `packages/skills/src/lint.ts`: the skills lint: frontmatter, folder name equals skill name, links. Key symbols: `lintSkills`, `parseFrontmatter`, `isValidSkillName`.
- `packages/skills/src/cli.ts`: `pnpm skills:lint`, over the package or a named folder. Key symbols: `lintSkills`.
- `packages/skills/src/instructions.ts`: a skill as the instructions file every run reads, on every engine, its linked files copied in. The optional `own` callback hands in the project's edited files with the folder each came from, and the text then names that folder in its second line; a file it has no edit of comes from the package. The test keeps every shipped phase under 17,000 characters. Key symbols: `phaseInstructions`.
- `packages/skills/src/text.ts`: the finish, knowledge and verdict rules, and the extraction and commit message prompts. The extraction prompt carries only the plain summary rule (`plainSummaryRule`), not the whole phase contract. Key symbols: `readFinishRules`, `readKnowledgeRules`, `readVerdictRules`, `plainSummaryRule`, `extractSummaryPrompt`, `commitMessagePrompt`, `threadTitlePrompt`.
- `packages/skills/src/project-files.ts`: where the shipped folders are, and the pointer files written into a project. Key symbols: `skillsFolder`, `packageFolder`, `pointerFile`, `AGENTS_MD`.
- `packages/skills/fixtures`: bad and valid skills for the lint tests.

## Scripts and examples

- `scripts/measure-walls.mjs`: a person runs it with their own logins. Runs each engine on PATH once inside the wall, with the runner's own argument builders and `wallSpecFor` for a QA-like read-only phase, in a temp project under the home folder (the macOS wall leaves the system temp folders writable), and writes a JSON report and a text summary of yes or no answers: started and logged in, the card note written, README unchanged, the `.env` marker and `known_hosts` absent, the state folders touched by mtime. Also Codex with `-c default_permissions`, Chromium started inside the wall with and without its own sandbox, the clone times of `--clone-repo`, bwrap and user namespaces, and `secret-tool` on WSL 2. Prints no output of the engine. How to run it is in `docs/engines/README.md`. Key symbols: `measureEngine`, `engineCommand`, `checks`, `snapshot`, `measureCodexProfile`, `measureChromium`, `measureClone`, `measureSecretTool`, `summary`.
- `scripts/measure-walls.test.ts`: runs the script with fake `claude` and `codex` binaries first on PATH, in the repository's `.vitest` folder. On macOS the fakes run inside the real `sandbox-exec` wall and the report says each forbidden write and read was refused. Key symbols: `FAKE`, `FAKE_CHROMIUM`, `MARKER`, `measure`.
- `apps/local/scripts/smoke.ts`: `NOLZU_FAKE_AGENT=1 pnpm smoke`. Copies the demo workspace, drives cards through the real loop, queue and scheduler on the fake engine, on one repository, two, and a workspace. Exits 1 on any mismatch. Key symbols: `createLoop`, `moveCard`, `check`.
- `apps/local/scripts/demo-setup.ts`: sets up a demo: a copy of the demo workspace as the project of a fresh data folder, with `APP-1` to `APP-3` in Inbox. `--split`, `--root-repo`, `--workspace`. Key symbols: `createLocalTicket`, `splitWorkspace`, `demoWorkspace`.
- `apps/local/scripts/demo-workspace.ts`: the demo repositories by URL. Key symbols: `demoWorkspace`, `demoUrl`.
- `apps/local/scripts/split-workspace.ts`: splits the demo into two repositories. Key symbols: `splitWorkspace`, `SPLIT_REPOS`.
- `apps/local/scripts/pack-check.ts`: `pnpm pack:check` after the build: packs the app, installs it into an empty prefix, starts it on the fake engine twice. `--release` (or `NOLZU_RELEASE=1`) also fails on a placeholder or development version, in the manifest and in what `nolzu --version` prints (`release`). Then it starts the packed app on Node 22 (`NOLZU_NODE22`, or the oldest nvm Node 22 at or above 22.16) and fails on a SQLite warning; with no Node 22 it skips that step and says so. Key symbols: `freePort`, `readRecord`, `release`, `found22`.
- `apps/local/scripts/pack-rules.ts`: the rules the tarball is held to. Key symbols: `outsideTheApp`, `isTestFile`, `inEngineFolder`, `isSourceMap`, `tracesMachine`, `loadsFromAnotherHost`, `releaseRefusal`, `publishRefusal`, `inCi`, `floorNode`.
- `apps/local/scripts/release-check.ts`: the `prepublishOnly` of the `nolzu` package, and step 7 of the release steps in `CONTRIBUTING.md`, because `npm publish` of a tarball runs no scripts: asks the built `dist/nolzu.js` for its version and refuses a publish of a `0.0.0` or `-dev` version, a prerelease on `latest`, a build that prints another version, and any publish under CI (`publishRefusal`, `inCi`). Key symbols: `publishRefusal`.
- `examples/recipe-workspace`: the demo project the smoke run, the demo setup and the fake scenarios use.
- `docs/stack-hook`: an example of own commands under Start the app, for every repository, with `compose.yaml` and `stack.sh`.
- `docs/extensions.md`: the feature interface for the paid package.
- `docs/review-comments.md`: answering review comments on a Delivery card's pull requests: Read comments, Run triage, Copy, Fix, then commit and push from Changes, and the board card's line.
- `docs/engines`: what each engine CLI was measured to print.
- `docs/board.svg`: the drawn Delivery board at the top of the README, in both themes, with the token values of `tokens.css`. Not a screenshot: `docs.test.ts` holds its columns and words to the board code.

## Tests

Run from the repository root: `corepack pnpm test`, or one file with
`corepack pnpm exec vitest run <path>`. Most test files sit beside the file they test, so
`board.ts` has `board.test.ts`. No test opens the live data folder (`vitest.setup.ts`), and every
test runs with a temp `HOME` and `TZ=UTC` (`vitest.global-setup.ts`).

The kinds, with one example each:

- `packages/core/src/board.test.ts`: pure tests. Import the function, call it, assert. Written first for a pure module. Key symbols: `canMove`, `phaseOnEnter`.
- `packages/core/src/node/cards.test.ts`: sqlite tests on a fresh database in a temp folder through `openDatabase`. Key symbols: `openDatabase`, `moveCard`.
- `packages/ui/src/board/Board.test.ts`: server render tests: `render` from `svelte/server`, then assert on the text. Key symbols: `render`, `boardFixture`.
- `packages/ui/src/board/Board.mount.test.ts`: mount tests in jsdom with the browser build of Svelte, for a click, an effect or the focus. Use `mounted` and `set` from `packages/ui/test/mounted.svelte.ts`. Key symbols: `mounted`, `boardFixture`.
- `apps/local/src/routes/t/[key]/stream.test.ts`: the card loads with git mocked to never answer (`vi.mock` of `$lib/changes.ts` and `$lib/overview.ts`): each load returns, and the git parts are still pending promises. Key symbols: `pending`, `never`.
- `apps/local/src/lib/Streamed.svelte.mount.test.ts`: the placeholder, then the answer, the error in place, a reload kept and another card dropped. A `.svelte` test file, so its snippet can update in an effect. Key symbols: `mounted`, `later`.
- `apps/local/src/lib/BoardPage.mount.test.ts`: an app component mounted for real with `mount` from svelte and the `$app` stubs. It writes `nowLive` the way the stream does. Key symbols: `mount`, `nowLive`, `drawnAt`.
- `apps/local/src/routes/api/cards/start.test.ts`: a route over a real loop and the fake engine. `vi.mock` swaps `loop()` for `createLoop(dir, { NOLZU_FAKE_AGENT: '1', NOLZU_FAKE_DELAY_SCALE: '1' })`, then calls `POST` with a plain `Request`. Key symbols: `createLoop`, `POST`, `held`.
- `apps/local/src/lib/loop.test.ts`: the loop itself, with `loop.engine.spawn` replaced to replay a named scenario. Key symbols: `createLoop`, `spawn`.
- `apps/local/src/routes/b/[board]/page.test.ts`: a page `load` called with a hand built event, and the page drawn with `render` and the `$app/paths` stub. Key symbols: `load`, `render`, `drawnAt`.
- `packages/runner/src/git/worktree.test.ts`: git tests on a real temp repository from `tempRepo`. Key symbols: `tempRepo`, `ensureWorktree`.
- `packages/runner/src/spawn.test.ts`: spawn tests against a real stub executable from `stubBinary`, with the wall mocked to start the engine as it came (CI has no bubblewrap). Key symbols: `stubBinary`.
- `packages/runner/src/qa-copy.test.ts`: the QA copy on real folders and a real git repository: the method per system, the plain copy when the clone is refused, uncommitted work in the copy, the start sweep. The copy deleted after a run that ends ready, fails or is stopped is in `apps/local/src/lib/loop.test.ts`. Key symbols: `makeQaCopy`, `sweepQaCopies`.
- `packages/runner/src/spawn-wall.test.ts`: the wall in spawn: the spec per phase and engine through `recordingWall`, a refusal that starts no process through `refusingWall`, the Linux rules with the system name swapped, and the stubs inside the real wall where this machine can start one, a QA stub on its copy among them. QA on a copy is run on all three engines with the refusal mocked off, so the copy is tested apart from the refusal. Key symbols: `recordingWall`, `refusingWall`.
- `docs.test.ts`: docs guards: links open, promises kept, CI steps listed in order, no internal ids on public pages, README and design system match the code (the README's four boards with their columns, its drawn board, and the website pages it may link, `SITE_ROUTES`). Key symbols: `PUBLIC`, `links`, `SITE_ROUTES`.
- `ui-copy.test.ts`: UI copy guards: no later version promised, every "at /route" names a real route. Key symbols: `ROOTS`, `withoutComments`.
- `public-text.test.ts`: every text file is public, so no note may need private material to read: no decision record, tracker id, design file, private doc or repository, private run or tool tag. Key symbols: `SHAPES`.
- `boundaries.test.ts`: proves the ESLint import boundaries refuse what they should. Key symbols: `ruleIds`.
- `amber.test.ts`: the amber color only where a run starts. Key symbols: `amber`.
- `ci.test.ts`: the guard in `.github/workflows/ci.yml`: no `workflow_run` or `pull_request_target` trigger, no `packages: write` and no publish step, every shared package private with no `publishConfig`, and no checkout keeps the token.
- `codemap.test.ts`: this file's guard. Key symbols: `ENTRY`, `SYMBOLS`.

The helpers:

- `packages/ui/test/mounted.svelte.ts`: mounts a component with props a test can change. Key symbols: `mounted`, `Mounted`.
- `packages/runner/test/temp-repo.ts`: a temp git repository with empty config and a fixed author. Key symbols: `tempRepo`, `TempRepo`.
- `packages/runner/test/stub-binary.ts`: a real executable standing in for an engine. Key symbols: `stubBinary`, `StubBinary`.
- `packages/runner/test/wall-double.ts`: wall doubles for `SpawnOptions.wall`: one that records the spec and starts the engine as it came, one that refuses. Key symbols: `recordingWall`, `refusingWall`.
- `test/stubs/app-paths.ts`: `$app/paths` for tests. Key symbols: `resolve`, `route`.
- `test/stubs/app-state.ts`: `$app/state` with a page a test fills in. Key symbols: `page`.
- `test/stubs/app-navigation.ts`: `goto` and `invalidateAll` that go nowhere. Key symbols: `goto`, `invalidateAll`.
- `test/stubs/app-forms.ts`: `use:enhance` that does nothing, `deserialize` as plain JSON for a mounted test's answer, and `applyAction` that does nothing. Key symbols: `enhance`, `deserialize`, `applyAction`.

## Main flows

### A drag onto an agent column

1. `BoardPage.svelte` builds `allowed` with `allowedFor`, which asks core's `canMove`. `Board.svelte` passes it to each `BoardColumn.svelte`, which shows `dropTarget` (what the drop starts, from the snapshot's `starts`) or `refuseDrop`.
2. On drop, `take` in `BoardColumn.svelte` calls `dropOutcome`. A refusal goes to `onRefuse`, and `BoardPage.svelte` raises it with `refusedDrop` into `toastStack`. Nothing is sent.
3. Otherwise `onMove` reaches `move` in `BoardPage.svelte`. A move into QA before the project answered how QA gets the app opens `QaAskDialog` through `askFirst` and sends nothing; Start QA posts the answer to `/api/qa-setup` and calls `move` again. Then `move` sets `follow`, marks the press (`pressed`), and calls `sendMove`, which posts `{ key, to }` to `/api/board/move` through `postWrite`.
4. `POST` in `apps/local/src/routes/api/board/move/+server.ts` calls `move` in `writes.ts`, which calls `moveTo`. `moveTo` reads the card's own board (`boardOf`) and calls `moveCard` in `packages/core/src/node/cards.ts`.
5. `moveCard` opens `BEGIN IMMEDIATE`, refuses a missing column or a move `canMove` refuses (409 with that sentence), asks `phaseOnEnter`, writes `recordMove` into `card_events`, sets the card `queued`, calls `bumpVersion`, and commits. Then it calls the injected start, which is `live.start(card, phase)`.
6. `start` in `loop.ts` calls `buildJob` (every refusal here fails the card with `failStart`, and `moveTo` answers 500), writes the run row with `startRun`, sets `log_path` and `cwd`, and calls `scheduler.add`, the project's view of the shared scheduler, which puts the project id on the job.
7. `createScheduler` puts the job through `enqueue` and `pick`, and when a slot and the lane are free (counting every project's runs) calls the `startJob` port of the job's own project. `startJob` calls `markRunning`, makes the checkout with `cardBranch` and `ensureWorktree`, then iterates `runWithRetries(engineFor(job.engine), job, signal, retries)`.
8. `runJob` in `packages/runner/src/run.ts` spawns the engine and parses each line (`parseClaudeLine` for Claude Code) into `RunEvent`s. Each event goes to `onEvent`, then `heard`, which updates `live` and tells the `onActivity` watchers.
9. When the engine ends, `startJob` folds the events with `foldRun`, saves the row (`saveRun`), records the summary (`recordSummary`, `recordPhaseSummary`), and lands the card with `landCard`, which moves it on when `chainTo` says so (`cardHolds` holds a wrap-up off the archive). The scheduler frees the slot and calls `onChange`, which runs `bumpVersion` and `changed`.
10. `events` in `apps/local/src/routes/api/board/stream.ts` listens through `onChange` and `onActivity`. On each change it sends a whole snapshot (`nowSnapshot` for `/api/now`) with the version as its id, and each activity as a named `activity` event.
11. In the browser `connectBoard` in `snapshot-client.ts` writes the new state into `nowLive`. `BoardPage.svelte` reads its board out of it, and `Board.svelte` draws the card in its new state. The answer to the POST only raises a toast (`answered`); the card moves through the stream.

### Add card with start

1. `AddCardDialog.svelte` reads the input with `readInput` and builds the body with `cardPayload`. `startOffer` says what the start button would run, or why a drop there is refused. It calls `onsubmit`.
2. `addCard` in `BoardPage.svelte` calls `addRequest` with the board on screen. It keeps `start: true` only when the second column starts a run and `refuseDrop` does not refuse it; otherwise it takes `start` out and keeps the refusal for the toast. Then `postCard` in `board-page.ts` posts to `/api/cards`.
3. `POST` in `apps/local/src/routes/api/cards/+server.ts` calls `postCard` in `create-card.ts` with `live.changed` and a start that is `moveTo(live, key, to)`.
4. `postCard` checks the body, then `createCardFrom`: `parseTicketRef` and `ticketLink` read the input, a GitHub ref is read through `gh` (`githubIssues` or `getPull`), a Jira key or link on a Jira project through `jiraReader` (the keychain is read here and nowhere earlier), and one transaction writes the card (`createLocalTicket` or `createCard`), the extra context and the links.
5. It calls `told` (the loop's `changed`), then `startFirst`, which finds the second stage with `nextStage` and calls the start. From here it is the drag flow from step 4: `moveTo`, `moveCard`, `start`.
6. The answer is 201 with `{ key, note }`, plus `refused` when the start was refused. The card stays in its first column then. `addedOf` and `addedToast` turn the answer into a toast when the dialog closes.

### A run that ends ready, and what Now shows

1. `foldRun` gives `cardState: 'ready'`. In `startJob`, `keepNamedRepos` keeps the repositories a refine or plan named, `saveRun` writes the row, and `recordSummary` writes `plain_summary` (from `summary.md`, or an `extract-summary` pass while the slot is held).
2. `recordPhaseSummary` posts what the phase said into its discussion thread. `landCard` sets the card `ready` (or moves it one stage on after a QA PASS, writing an `auto` move). The card goes into `chaining`.
3. The scheduler's `onChange` bumps the version, calls `changed`, and asks `chainOn` once the card left the queue. `chainOn` moves a small Delivery card from Plan to Implement through `moveTo` when the board is in `chainBoards`, or writes `chain_refused` on the run.
4. `/api/now` sends `nowSnapshot`: every board that is on, and for each card that passes `waitingOnMe` a `waitingExtra` with its time, its plain summary and any chain refusal.
5. `waitingItems` in `now.ts` sorts them, `kindOf` names a ready card `ready` (or `qa-failed` on a FAIL verdict, or `tasks-done` for an epic whose every task is done). `NowItem.svelte` shows `whenLine` ("Plan done 3m ago", or "Review · waiting 3m" for a card a run moved on to a person's column), the summary, and `startStep` ("Approve and start Implement" with the drop's own command line, or Fix it on a QA failure) and `backStep`.
6. The rail count is `waitingCount` over the same snapshot, the current project's only, so it never disagrees with Now.

### A card page tab reads its data

1. `/t/<key>` redirects to `/t/<key>/overview` (`TAB_REDIRECT`).
2. `load` in `apps/local/src/routes/t/[key]/+layout.server.ts` calls `ticketLayout`, which reads the card and `tabsFor` its board and stage, and `runOnOffer`.
3. `load` in `apps/local/src/routes/t/[key]/[tab]/+page.server.ts` checks the tab with `ticketTab`, then reads only what that tab shows, git and project commands as streamed promises, for example `changeGroups` (git) for `changes`, `logFor` for `log`, `discussionFor` and then `withTools` (each agent message's run log, streamed) for `discussion`, `settingsFor`, `cardReposFor` and `cardChain` for `settings`, `cardPullRows` for `prs`.
4. `+page.svelte` draws the tab component from `packages/ui/src/ticket`. Its writes go to `/api/card/<key>/*`, then `invalidateAll` reads the tab again. The card's state and busy state come from the Now stream, or the board stream the layout opened with `streamAddress` for a board that is off. On the overview the layout draws the right column beside the tab: `DecisionPanel` from the snapshot, and the trail and the facts from the overview's page data, or the record on a closed card. A closed card's Overview asks `/api/card/<key>/pr` for the pull request state after the page has drawn, so gh never holds the page load.

### A setting written and read back

1. A form on `/settings/<section>` posts to an action in `apps/local/src/routes/settings/[section]/+page.server.ts`, for example `runs`, which calls `postAgents`.
2. `postSetting` in `settings-page.ts` reads one field, and `saveAgentField` turns it into a typed value. `storeSetting` asks `settingProblem` and calls `writeSetting`.
3. `writeSetting` in `packages/core/src/node/settings-store.ts` validates with `validateSetting`, writes the JSON into `settings` (`machine.settings` for a key in `MACHINE_KEYS`), and calls `bumpVersion`.
4. A setting the board draws also needs `changed` on the loop (the `runs` action does it for the quiet time), or open boards keep the old value until the next change. The slot count lives in the scheduler too, so the `runs` action hands a saved one to `scheduler.resize`, which every project's runs share.
5. Every reader calls `getSetting(db, key)`, which reads the row through `readSetting` and falls back to `DEFAULTS` for a missing or broken value. A new key goes into `Settings`, `DEFAULTS` and the validators in `packages/core/src/settings.ts`, and into `MACHINE_KEYS` when it follows the machine rather than the project.

### A migration added

1. Append one SQL string at the end of `migrations` in `packages/core/src/node/migrations.ts`, with a comment on why. Never edit a released entry.
2. `openDatabase` in `packages/core/src/node/db.ts` runs `migrate`, on `machine.db` first and then on `nolzu.db`, which applies each entry above `PRAGMA user_version` in its own `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` and stamps the new version. A database newer than the code refuses to open with "Update Nolzu to open it".
3. Read and write the new column where its rows live: `packages/core/src/node/cards.ts` for cards, `runs.ts` for runs, and `snapshot.ts` when the board shows it. A column the hub needs also goes into `hub-sync.ts`.
4. `packages/core/src/node/db.test.ts` checks a fresh database ends at `migrations.length` and a failed statement leaves the version unchanged. Add a test that opens an older database and reads the new column.

## Where a change usually goes

- A new board or stage: `OTHER_BOARDS` or `deliveryBoard` in `packages/core/src/board.ts`, `BOARD_KINDS` in `packages/protocol/src/board.ts`, a skill and a fake scenario for a new phase, and `tabsFor`. The board test holds the stage lists written out.
- A new phase: `ACTIONS` in `models.ts`, `PHASE_POLICIES`, a folder in `packages/skills/skills`, `packages/runner/fake/<phase>.ndjson`, and a `scenarioDelays` entry.
- A new card write: a function in `packages/core/src/node/cards.ts` that calls `bumpVersion`, a route under `apps/local/src/routes/api/card/[key]`, and `changed` on the loop.
- A new run outcome a card shows: `foldRun`, a column, `saveRun` or a new saver in `runs.ts`, `boardSnapshot`, and `CardView`.
- A new setting: `Settings`, `DEFAULTS`, the validator, then the section reader and saver in `apps/local/src/lib`.
- A new hub job: a name in `SERVER_MESSAGES`, a branch in `run` in `hub-jobs.ts` that calls a writer a click already reaches. A new frame: `RUNNER_MESSAGES`, a reader in `hub.ts`, a line in `SYNCED_DATA`, and the sender in `hub-sync.ts`. Both need a matching change on the server.
- A new key: an entry in `KEYS` in `packages/ui/src/keymap.ts`, and the action on the screen's `pageKeys`.
- A new component state: the pure rule in a `.ts` beside the component with its test, then the `.svelte` file only draws.

## Traps

- Run Vitest from the root. Inside a package it misses the Svelte plugin and the inline projects, and blames the component.
- Use `corepack pnpm`, not a standalone `pnpm`, which may be another version.
- Svelte 5 trims a space at the edge of an element. Put the space between nodes, as `BoardCard.svelte` does, or a screen reader hears two words as one.
- `@nolzu/ui`, `@nolzu/protocol` and `@nolzu/core` are read by the server too. Do not rename or remove an export; add a new one beside it.
- Migrations are append only. A released entry runs on databases that already exist. The same holds for `machineMigrations`.
- `writer` and `project_registry` live only in `machine.db`, so the bare name reads the machine's table on a project connection. A table of the same name in a project database would hide it.
- A machine key's row is in `machine.settings`, not in `settings`. A test that reads or writes the table by hand names `machine.settings` for a key in `MACHINE_KEYS`; a row it puts in the project's table is read only while `machine.db` has none.
- Busy state comes from the snapshot, never from a click's promise. A POST answer only raises a toast; the stream moves the card. A direct git action, such as a commit, shows its own pending state.
- `loop.ts` and anything it imports cannot import the `@nolzu/ui` barrel: `smoke.ts` runs the loop under plain Node, which cannot load a `.svelte` file. Use a pure subpath such as `@nolzu/ui/qa`, `@nolzu/ui/comments` or `@nolzu/ui/knowledge`.
- `loop.ts` imports `moveTo` from `apps/local/src/routes/api/board/writes.ts`. Keep `writes.ts` free of SvelteKit imports, or the loop stops loading in Node.
- There are two sqlite connections in the server: the loop's (`loop().db`) and `database()` in `db.ts`. A write through either moves the version, but only `changed` on the loop tells the open streams.
- Stage ids repeat across boards (`inbox`, `refine`, `plan`, `review`, `wrapup`). Read a card's stage against its own board with `boardOf` or `boardOfCard`, never against Delivery.
- A card left `queued` with no job refuses every move. Anything that can throw after a card is set queued must fail it with the reason (`failStart`, the catch in `moveCard`).
- `startJob` must not throw: the scheduler swallows it and the card would stay as it was. Failures become a `failed` event.
- `buildJob` is the one gate. A drop, a retry, Run on, an answer, a message and the chain all pass it, so a new refusal goes there and not in one caller.
- The fake engine forces `~/.nolzu-fake` and ignores `NOLZU_DATA_DIR`. Tests pass the env object to `createLoop`; nothing sets `process.env.NOLZU_FAKE_AGENT`.
- One loop per project, cached in `open` by the data folder and the project; the first project has the data folder's key whether it is named by its id or not. A test calls `close` in `afterEach`, or a later `createLoop` on the same folder gets the old loop back with its database.
- `database()` and `loop()` read the current project from `machine.db` on every call. A test that makes a second project and sets `currentProject` moves every route to it, and on Free (no held document with `projects`) a route that names another project throws.
- `packages/ui` and `packages/skills` may import only protocol, and core may import only protocol. The ESLint boundaries in `eslint.config.js` fail the lint, not the tests.
- A key typed by a person or read from a ticket system is checked once, in `createCard` (`cardKeyProblem`). A key with a slash gets no folder, which is why GitHub cards are keyed `repo#n`.
- The CI dash check fails on an em or en dash in any `*.md`, `*.svelte` or `*.html` file. `docs.test.ts` also refuses internal ids on public pages.
- The commands in `AGENTS.md` do not run gitleaks, which CI runs over the whole history.
